I don’t know if that makes sense, but it’s been hard to just feel settled.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a specific stressful event or major life change?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was something that kind of set it off. It’s been about nine months now, around when the condo loan started moving toward foreclosure. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but that’s when I noticed I wasn’t really settling back down the way I used to. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever get sudden surges of intense fear or physical panic that peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like a sudden “attack” that peaks really fast, at least. I do get anxious, but it feels more like it builds or just kind of stays there, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you mainly feel this anxiety when you might be watched, judged, or evaluated by other people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s mainly that, no. I mean, I don’t love being judged—who does—but the anxious feeling doesn’t seem limited to situations with other people watching me. It can happen even when I’m just by myself. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about many different parts of your life, not just the condo foreclosure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has kind of spread out beyond the condo. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m worrying about everything all the time, but it jumps around a lot—like whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up on a support call at work, or how I’m ever going to save money and move forward. So… yes, it’s not just the foreclosure, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I’ve felt kind of worn down and discouraged at times, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being “down a lot.” I’m trying to keep functioning, so… maybe a little, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure situation were fully resolved, do you think this anxiety would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. It would definitely help. But… I don’t think it would mostly go away completely, because my worry doesn’t really stay on the condo anymore. It kind of jumps around—to whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’ll make a mistake on a support call, or how I’m ever going to save money or move forward. So I guess… no, not entirely. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, is it hard for you to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I try to be reasonable with myself, like, “You already checked the spreadsheet,” or “You already sent the email, it’s fine.” But the thought doesn’t really stop there. It kind of keeps looping, and then one “what if” turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as hard to control, but… it feels like I can’t always shut it off when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s actually a big part of why I came in. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I feel wound up a lot of the time, like my body is sort of bracing for the next bad thing.\n\nEven if I’m just sitting at my desk, or I’m supposed to be relaxing—like at dance night—I can still feel kind of on edge. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Not like a sudden “attack” that peaks really fast, at least. I do get anxious, but it feels more like it builds or just kind of stays there, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you mainly feel this anxiety when you might be watched, judged, or evaluated by other people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s mainly that, no. I mean, I don’t love being judged—who does—but the anxious feeling doesn’t seem limited to situations with other people watching me. It can happen even when I’m just by myself. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about many different parts of your life, not just the condo foreclosure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has kind of spread out beyond the condo. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m worrying about everything all the time, but it jumps around a lot—like whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up on a support call at work, or how I’m ever going to save money and move forward. So… yes, it’s not just the foreclosure, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I’ve felt kind of worn down and discouraged at times, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being “down a lot.” I’m trying to keep functioning, so… maybe a little, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure situation were fully resolved, do you think this anxiety would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. It would definitely help. But… I don’t think it would mostly go away completely, because my worry doesn’t really stay on the condo anymore. It kind of jumps around—to whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’ll make a mistake on a support call, or how I’m ever going to save money or move forward. So I guess… no, not entirely. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, is it hard for you to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I try to be reasonable with myself, like, “You already checked the spreadsheet,” or “You already sent the email, it’s fine.” But the thought doesn’t really stop there. It kind of keeps looping, and then one “what if” turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as hard to control, but… it feels like I can’t always shut it off when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s actually a big part of why I came in. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I feel wound up a lot of the time, like my body is sort of bracing for the next bad thing.\n\nEven if I’m just sitting at my desk, or I’m supposed to be relaxing—like at dance night—I can still feel kind of on edge. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Um… I wouldn’t say depressed most days, no. I mean, I get discouraged and kind of worn down sometimes, but I don’t know that I’d call it sad or depressed in that way.\n\nI don’t want to understate it, but… it feels more like stress than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting much less interest or enjoyment from things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I still like the things I usually like… maybe I’m not always as relaxed during them as I want to be, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest or enjoyment.\n\nLike, if I make myself go do something, I can usually still enjoy it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this anxious more days than not for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not every single day all day, but more days than not, yes.\n\nIt’s been about nine months, I guess—since the condo loan started moving toward foreclosure. I kept thinking it would calm down once I got a handle on things, but it hasn’t really. Does that count as long enough?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean, I don’t love being judged—who does—but the anxious feeling doesn’t seem limited to situations with other people watching me. It can happen even when I’m just by myself. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about many different parts of your life, not just the condo foreclosure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has kind of spread out beyond the condo. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m worrying about everything all the time, but it jumps around a lot—like whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up on a support call at work, or how I’m ever going to save money and move forward. So… yes, it’s not just the foreclosure, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly. I’ve felt kind of worn down and discouraged at times, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being “down a lot.” I’m trying to keep functioning, so… maybe a little, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure situation were fully resolved, do you think this anxiety would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. It would definitely help. But… I don’t think it would mostly go away completely, because my worry doesn’t really stay on the condo anymore. It kind of jumps around—to whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’ll make a mistake on a support call, or how I’m ever going to save money or move forward. So I guess… no, not entirely. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, is it hard for you to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I try to be reasonable with myself, like, “You already checked the spreadsheet,” or “You already sent the email, it’s fine.” But the thought doesn’t really stop there. It kind of keeps looping, and then one “what if” turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as hard to control, but… it feels like I can’t always shut it off when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s actually a big part of why I came in. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I feel wound up a lot of the time, like my body is sort of bracing for the next bad thing.\n\nEven if I’m just sitting at my desk, or I’m supposed to be relaxing—like at dance night—I can still feel kind of on edge. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Um… I wouldn’t say depressed most days, no. I mean, I get discouraged and kind of worn down sometimes, but I don’t know that I’d call it sad or depressed in that way.\n\nI don’t want to understate it, but… it feels more like stress than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting much less interest or enjoyment from things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I still like the things I usually like… maybe I’m not always as relaxed during them as I want to be, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest or enjoyment.\n\nLike, if I make myself go do something, I can usually still enjoy it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this anxious more days than not for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not every single day all day, but more days than not, yes.\n\nIt’s been about nine months, I guess—since the condo loan started moving toward foreclosure. I kept thinking it would calm down once I got a handle on things, but it hasn’t really. Does that count as long enough?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling easily fatigued lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anyone would feel with everything going on. I can still get through my day, mostly. So… maybe a little, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Sometimes coworkers have had to help pick up the slack, which I feel bad about.\n\nAnd socially… I’ve skipped a few dance nights and mentoring sessions because I just felt too overwhelmed to show up. I know that probably isn’t ideal. So, yes, I guess it is getting in the way more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you still get this anxious even at times when there isn’t anything specific going wrong or any immediate problem to think about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to say it’s for no reason, because I can usually find something to attach it to if I think hard enough. But even when nothing is actually happening, I still feel kind of wound up, like my body is bracing for the next bad thing.\n\nEven just sitting at my desk, or when I’m supposed to be enjoying dance night, there’s this tension there. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean, but… yes, I think it happens even without an immediate problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things that are unrelated to the foreclosure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, the foreclosure is still the big thing, obviously, so I don’t want to act like I’m just worrying out of nowhere.\n\nBut it doesn’t really stay on the condo anymore. It’ll jump to whether my cousin is upset with me, or whether I’m going to make some mistake on a support call, or how I’m ever supposed to save money and move forward. It’s like my brain just keeps finding another thing to latch onto. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has the anxiety stayed closely connected to the foreclosure situation, or does it now feel more separate from that stress?\n\nPatient: I guess it started with the foreclosure, but… no, it doesn’t feel like it stays neatly connected to that anymore.\n\nIt kind of jumps around now. Like I’ll be worrying whether my cousin is upset with me, then whether I’m going to mess up on a support call, then how I’m ever supposed to save money or move forward. I know some of that is probably normal to think about, but it feels like my brain keeps looking for the next thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your anxiety is mostly tied to the foreclosure stress, or does it feel like a general pattern of worry now?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s more of a general pattern now, if I’m being honest. The condo is still part of it, but my worry doesn’t really stay there anymore.\n\nIt’ll jump around — like, whether my cousin is upset with me, or whether I’m going to make a mistake on a support call, or how I’m ever supposed to save money and move forward with my life. I know those are real things to think about, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it feels like my mind keeps finding something new to worry about."
